Shari Rosenblum, Disney TV Studios’ PR Lead, Exits the Company

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

The senior vice president of publicity has been with Disney since it purchased Twentieth Century Fox.

Shari Rosenblum has exited Disney after six years with the company, The Hollywood Reporter has learned. Rosenblum accepted a voluntary buyout package — Disney has been offering eligible executives a VERO (Voluntary Early Retirement Offer).

Rosenblum had been senior vice president of communications and publicity at Disney’s TV studios. She came over with Twentieth Century Fox Television in 2019 (Rosenblum had been with the Fox TV studio since 2005). Rosenblum led campaigns for series like Modern Family, Homeland and The Dropout. She is an alum of the University of Arizona and Boston University. Related Stories Business Pixar Taps Jonathan Garson as President With Jim Morris Stepping Back to President Emeritus General News Johnny Depp on His One Condition to Return as Jack Sparrow for 'Pirates of the Caribbean 6'

Disney has undergone massive layoffs lately. CEO Josh D’Amaro cut hundreds of jobs in September. The latest round follows a staff reduction in July and a large layoff in April. There have been even larger cuts in recent years.

“I’m moving on from my Disney-Fox career with nothing but extreme gratitude.” Rosenblum said. “I’m grateful to Dana [Walden] and Gary [Newman] for taking a chance on an ambitious young publicist over two decades ago, and to the many mentors and champions I’ve had along the way, including Shannon Ryan and Candice Ashton. It’s been a privilege to tell stories for our industry’s top storytellers, especially during this last stretch, with Karey Burke and the incredible executives at 20th TV. I feel beyond fortunate to have worked alongside the most talented and brilliant minds in our industry, and have a deep appreciation for the business of television, the people in it, the shows they make, and the journalists who report on it all.”

“Shari has led PR for Disney Television Studios’ 20th Television and 20th Television Animation with equal parts brilliance and grace,” Ashton said, in part, in an internal email (obtained by THR). “Few people have shaped the voice and reputation of our studio the way she has. Shari is a consummate pro, and an advocate and champion for so many of our teams and creative partners. She has also been a wonderful partner through every curveball along the way, and we’ve shared many laughs together that I will miss very much.”

Rosenblum serves on the Board of P.S. Arts (since 2019), was a member of the Local 600 International Cinematographers & Publicists Guild from 2005-2022, and was a recipient of the Maxwell Weinberg Showmanship Award for Television for her work on Homeland.

Wouter Vrancken's Hearts message after Swiss setback - 'we have to stick together'

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Wouter Vrancken insists Hearts will have to stick together following their self-inflicted 3-1 Conference League defeat by Thun in Switzerland.

The Scottish outfit, without 10 players through various injuries, fell behind in the 22nd minute when defender Jamie McCart knocked the ball into his own net.

Did you know with a Digital Subscription to The Scotsman, you can get unlimited access to the website including our premium content, as well as benefiting from fewer ads, loyalty rewards and much more.

Thun doubled their lead in the 33rd minute through striker Brighton Labeau after a poor attempted clearance from captain Stephen Kingsley – who went off later with an injury – and then Furkan Dursun grabbed a third in the 56th minute.

Jambos defender Stuart Findlay then gave some unrealised hope of a comeback in the 82nd minute when he headed in from close range.

After three Conference League fixtures Hearts have one point out of nine and have won just two of their last 10 fixtures in all competitions.

Vrancken told TNT Sports: “Like I said already to the guys, we have to stick together now. We know which situation we’re in with all the circumstances.

“And there’s only one thing that you have to do now and that is stick together and keep working hard. In general, it was not good enough in quality and can you blame them?

“Yeah, on one hand you can because you want to always expect the best and want the best. On the other hand, I’m still proud of what they did already in the run-up games.”

Kingsley was replaced in the second half by 19-year-old Matthew Gillies, called back from a loan spell at Stenhousemuir amid the club’s injury crisis, and Vrancken admitted there was an element of risk playing him on the artificial surface which the Jambos boss had criticised.

He said: “We talked it through before the game of course, a few days before the game. And yeah, it’s always a risk, especially when it’s another surface, and especially when it’s bad like that, but he was confident in playing and that’s where we are.”

Hearts’ next Conference League game is against Ligue 1 side in Monaco at Tynecastle on November 26 but Vrancken is more concerned about the home William Hill Premiership game against Falkirk on Sunday.

He said: “Monaco’s a really strong side, maybe the strongest side we will face in Europe. So we will see when we get there, because I think there are a lot of important games before that, especially on Sunday.”

Harry Kane’s sensational return to Tottenham remains possible thanks to clause despite new contract

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Tottenham have retained their first-option matching rights on Harry Kane, despite the England captain signing a new mega-contract with Bayern Munich that makes him Europe’s highest-paid player.

The 33-year-old striker has committed his future to the Bundesliga giants following his historic Ballon d’Or win, with his new deal reportedly making him the highest-paid footballer in Europe.

Despite Kane tying his immediate future to Bayern, The Telegraph reveals that Tottenham’s leverage has not changed.

The report explains that as part of Kane’s £100 million transfer to Bayern in 2023, Spurs secured first-option matching rights that would allow them to match an offer accepted by Bayern from another Premier League club.

More importantly, the agreement is between the two clubs and understood to apply throughout Kane’s time at Bayern, rather than being tied to the duration of his original contract.

That means the striker’s latest extension does not automatically invalidate Tottenham’s rights.

While media continues to link the player with a return at every opportunity, the immediate chances of Kane returning to North London remain slim given his success and comfort in Munich.

However, the dream of a sensational one last ride in English football remains alive.

Kane sits on 213 Premier League goals, just 47 goals behind Alan Shearer’s all-time record of 260.

Having paid tribute to Spurs during his Ballon d’Or acceptance speech, a sensational return to the North London club to break the ultimate goalscoring record could prove tempting before his career concludes.

UEL | AZ Alkmaar 1-2 Juventus: Special K success in Alkmaar

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Juventus secured an impressive 2-1 win away to AZ Alkmaar, who were unbeaten at home in Europe for over three years, as Teun Koopmeiners and Randal Kolo Muani kept strong Europa League form going.

The Bianconeri had a much-needed 2-0 victory over Napoli on Sunday and were unbeaten in the Europa League with two wins and a 2-2 draw against Rennes. Injuries continued to pile up, as Weston McKennie joined Kenan Yildiz, Manuel Locatelli, Nico Gonzalez, Francisco Conceicao, Zeki Celik, Khephren Thuram and Kamil Grabara on the treatment table, so Jeff Ekhator was added to the UEFA list.

Teun Koopmeiners faced his younger brother Peer, with both coming up through the AZ youth academy, and the crowd had a pre-match banner depicting them as children. The Dutch side lost the opening Europa League fixture at Sunderland, then beat Hapoel Beer Sheva and Ararat-Armenia, but were unbeaten in 17 UEFA home fixtures stretching back to October 2023.

Jeroen Zoet, Jizz Hornkamp, Wouter Goes and Stije Resink were out of action, but Kees Smit was back in the squad for the first time since September 20. They lost their last two Eredivisie games without finding the net.

Randal Kolo Muani’s angled drive flashed across the face of goal to skim the far post, and the ex-PSG man was instrumental in the opening goal.

Kolo Muani flicked the ball up over a defender to get down the right and pulled back to the edge of the area, where Teun Koopmeiners was ready to sweep in with the inside of the left boot. The midfielder did not celebrate against his hometown club, with his brother captaining them.

Things went from bad to worse for the hosts, who were forced into a change for the Lewis Schouten injury moments later.

Kerim Alajbegovic’s scorcher skimmed the crossbar at the far top corner from just inside the box, but AZ Alkmaar started to push back, Guglielmo Vicario right behind the deflected Dave Kwakman strike.

Juventus doubled their lead when a high Kolo Muani press forced Mees de Wit into an error, Nick Woltemade returning the pass for the Frenchman to bend the left-foot finish into the far bottom corner.

Alajbegovic hesitated when Teun Koopmeiners intercepted a mistaken Jari De Busser pass and the chance went begging, then Federico Gatti sprinted from his own half only to over-hit the final ball for Kolo Muani.

Smit came off the bench and saw his effort charged down by Lloyd Kelly, then fired another very promising attempt over from 12 yards while the hosts had penalty appeals rejected when Elijah Dijkstra went down under pressure from Kelly.

AZ Alkmaar did eventually get back into the game when Ayoub Oufkir robbed Kelly at the touchline and sent Dijkstra down the right, pulling back for Mexx Meerdink to tap in from close range.

Suddenly the hosts were fired up and had the ball in the net again during added time, but Meerdink was offside when his header forced an extraordinary Guglielmo Vicario save, so the rebound from Mees de Wit did not count.

The pressure was on to the final whistle, including a Smit free kick into the crowd that could’ve gone anywhere, but Juve held on.

T Koopmeiners 19 (J), Kolo Muani 37 (J), Meerdink 88 (A)

Nasdaq drops into correction territory — here’s what typically happens next

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

The Nasdaq Composite plunged into correction territory on Thursday. History shows more selling can follow in the near term, but a recovery eventually takes hold three months later.

The tech-heavy index slid more than 5% on Thursday, closing more than 11% below its record reached last month. On Wall Street, any decline greater than 10% is a correction. A 20% pullback qualifies as a bear market.

Across 10 previous correction episodes over the last decade, selloffs in 2025, 2022, 2020 and 2018 were followed by further rough selling over the next one month. But the Nasdaq was back to rally mode again after six months for all those occasions except one: 2022.

Overall, the Nasdaq gained an average of 9.41% from the correction bottom over the following three months and 17.03% over six months.

The January 2022 correction was the one that offered a harsher outcome. The Nasdaq lost another 18.3% over the next six months and was down 24.3% a year later.

The catalyst for this correction was Democrats sweeping the House and Senate in Tuesday's midterm elections. The party also made significant gains in Texas, where Gina Hinojosa won the race for governor after campaigning on an immediate moratorium for data center development.

Investors are now weighing whether tighter state-level restrictions could slow data center construction, putting pressure on companies tied to the artificial intelligence trade.

Frank Cappelleri, founder of CappThesis, pointed to past midterm election cycles for guidance and found that points to more volatility in the near term as well.

The chart analyst said the Nasdaq was higher 12 months after the election in all but one of 13 midterm cycles since 1974.

"The caveat is that the path can be a volatile one," he said.

Cappelleri pointed to 2018, when the Nasdaq rallied the day after Democrats won the House. A sharp December sell-off followed, but the index was ultimately about 15% higher a year after the election, he said.

Chip stocks tumbled Thursday, with the iShares Semiconductor ETF (SOXX) falling 12% and for its worst day since the Covid-19 breakout of March 2020.

— CNBC's Fred Imbert contributed to the report

One of My Least Favorite Politicians Lost an Election This Week. I Can’t Stop Laughing About It.

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

This guy was an ur-Trumper before Trump, and I’m so glad we’re rid of him.

This one is for me. Pardon me while I bust a gut.

Kris Kobach of Kansas—and never has a phrase with three K’s been more apt—is one of the founding weasels of the modern voter-suppression effort. If there is an odious policy position concocted since 2008, Kobach has run the laboratory that produced it. Imaginary voter fraud? Kobach led the farcical election-integrity commission during Trump I that fell apart after a contentious 2017 hearing in New Hampshire at which Kobach’s contentions were disemboweled by Maine secretary of state Matthew Dunlap. Draconian immigration policies? As Kansas secretary of state, Kobach tried to initiate a “proof of citizenship” law that lined him up on the wrong side of the League of Women Voters. Outright racism? Kobach told a radio program that the Obama administration was not far from a call for an end to all prosecutions of African Americans. If there was an ur-Trumper before Trump, it was Kris Kobach.

I thought we were rid of him when he ran for governor in 2018 and lost. I thought we were rid of him when he ran for the Senate in 2020 and lost. I thought we were rid of him when he got tangled up with Steve Bannon’s Build the Wall scam. But he was like the shingles virus in the body politic. He got elected Kansas attorney general in 2022.

And, on Tuesday, and extending into Thursday afternoon, it looks as though Kris Kobach might have lost another election. May it be his last.

Live Nation Reports $10.1 Billion In Revenue In Jump From Last Year

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

"Artists are taking their music to more fans in more places than ever, and fans are showing up in record numbers," CEO Michael Rapino said in a statement.

Live Nation posted a record-high $10.1 billion in revenue for the company’s third quarter, the live music giant reported on Thursday, buoyed by the company’s growing international business.

The $10.1 billion figure marks a 19 percent increase from 2025, while the company reported $892 million in operating income, a 13 percent jump from last year. Fan attendance overall grew 19 percent to 61 million people, Live Nation said, with international attendance growing 29 percent and accounting for 60 percent of the company’s total fan growth.

The company’s concerts division reported $8.7 billion in revenue for the quarter, with about $602.9 million in income, while ticketing reported $870 million in revenue and over $315 million in operating income. Live Nation’s sponsorship and advertising division posted nearly $506 million in revenue and over $350 million in income.

Live Nation also posted $3.8 billion in deferred revenue, an eight percent increase from last year.

“In a world of endless screens and AI-generated everything, the one thing that can’t be replicated is being there,” Live Nation CEO Michael Rapino said in a statement, adding that the company expects 180 million fans in attendance through 2026. “Artists are taking their music to more fans in more places than ever, and fans are showing up in record numbers.“

It’s Time to Talk About Classism in the Art World

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

In his memoiristic new book, photographer Mark Alice Durant weaves his personal experiences into a larger rebuke of the economic inequality that governs the art world.

For something that determines so much — about the art we make, the art we get to see or not, the art that may never be made at all — the issue of class is rarely discussed in the art world. Breaking the silence, artist and writer Mark Alice Durant speaks up in his memoir-cum-rebuke, Confessions of a Working Class Artist (2026). It reads both like a Künstlerroman and an updated version of the seminal guide for the aspiring artist, Rainer Maria Rilke’s Letters to a Young Poet (1929), for our dystopian neo-Gilded Age.

For Durant, also the author of the moving photographic memoir Summer of the White Fox, and After (2024) and publisher at Saint Lucy Books, which released both titles, choosing to become an artist was an act of self-exile. He writes that he psychically uprooted himself from the working-class precincts of Boston where he grew up as well as from his extended family, with the exception of his mother, unusually open-minded and supportive of her son. Perhaps she saw herself in his youthful gift of a framed reproduction of Jules Breton’s 1884 painting of a field worker, “The Song of the Lark.” After deciding on this path, Durant writes, “I could never return home on some level.”

Sony revives the original Walkman headphones, adds USB-C

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Sony has brought back the MDR-3-series headphones that debuted in 1979 alongside the Walkman TPS-L2 cassette player.

Originally called the “MDR-3L2,” the first Walkman headphones were known for their sleek, adjustable gray plastic headband, which extended via metal sliders and ended in signature orange, circular ear cushions. The lightweight, on-ear headphones are a contrast to the bulky headsets that are popular these days, which use wide, cushioned headbands for long-term comfort and large earcups that can support big drivers and are often heavily padded for noise isolation.

The new MDR-3R (note the slightly different name) headphones look just like the originals and come with orange and blue ear cushions.

New headphones are often wireless these days, but the MDR-3R continues the wired tradition of the MDR-3, with an essential twist: a USB-C connector.

“The MDR-3R has been redesigned for modern use while preserving the experience value of freely taking music out into the world, which the original MDR-3 helped popularize,” Sony’s announcement says.

For better or worse, the new headphones also use “the same 23 mm driver units as the MDR-3 to reproduce sound quality close to the original,” per Sony. Audiophiles will prefer something more advanced, perhaps with additional acoustic features. A review of the new headphones from Gizmodo says that the MDR-3R headphones “hold their own,” have “bright and clear” mids and highs, and good stereo separation. However, we don’t expect strong bass.

Unlike their predecessor, the MDR-3R has an in-line remote control and an integrated microphone. Without noise-canceling, though, the microphone isn’t ideal for calls in noisy environments.

But nostalgic gear isn’t about having top-of-the-line capabilities; it’s about showing off your love of old-school tech while enjoying familiar technologies that have reliably served their functions for years.

The MDR-3R may also capitalize on surging renewed interest in wired headphones and even physical media and Walkmans. Today’s Walkmans aren’t cassette players, of course, but are Android-powered, touchscreen media players.

Instagram launches free Edits video editor for Mac with 4K exports

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Instagram announced on Thursday that it is bringing Edits, its short-form video editing app, to desktop on Mac.The app is free to download, but it works only on Macs with Apple Silicon chips, from the M1 onwards, and users need an Instagram account to sign in.

Instagram first launched Edits on phones in April 2025 as a rival to CapCut, the editing app owned by TikTok’s parent company, ByteDance. More than 50 million creators now use the mobile version, the company told Gulf News.

The Mac app looks similar to the mobile one, but it gives editors more room and more tools for complex projects.

These include a timeline with several tracks, keyframes for smooth transitions, audio waveforms, and colour wheels and curves for adjusting the look of a clip.

Users can also import their own fonts and colour presets, known as LUTs, and set their own keyboard shortcuts.

In addition, the app comes with Instagram’s library of licensed, royalty-free music, hundreds of effects and unlimited automatic captions.

Once a video is finished, creators can post it straight to Instagram from the Mac, with tags and location, instead of moving the file to their phone first. Alternatively, they can export it in vertical or horizontal formats, in up to 4K and without a watermark.

The desktop app also works with AI agents. By switching on the Model Context Protocol, a standard that lets AI assistants use other software, creators can ask an agent to handle parts of the editing for them.

Matthew Simari, who leads Edits, said users can connect the agent they prefer, including Meta’s own Muse assistant. For example, a creator could ask Muse for a rough cut and then finish the edit by hand.

Some features are still missing, however. Users cannot yet work on the same project on both their phone and their Mac, although saved audio and text styles from the mobile app already carry over.

There is also no version for Windows yet, and Instagram said it plans to update the Mac app every week.

Lindsey Vonn Is Headed for Her 9th Surgery. She’s Still Thinking About Skiing.

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Lndsey Vonn inspects the slope before the second official training for the women's downhill event at the Tofane Alpine Skiing Centre during Milano Cortina 2026 Winter Olympic Games (Photo: Stefano Rellandini/AFP via Getty Images)

Lindsey Vonn is just days away from her ninth surgery in as many months, and the three-time Olympic medalist is still thinking about skiing.

On November 9, Vonn, 42, is heading into what’s slated to be the final surgery on her left leg following her severe crash during the Milano Cortina 2026 Winter Olympics in February. In an exclusive interview with Forbes, the alpine skier detailed her upcoming surgery.

“The plan is to remove all of the hardware, finally fix the ACL, and hopefully that’s my last surgery,” she told Forbes.

In February 2026, Vonn started the Olympic downhill race despite rupturing the ACL in her left knee just days earlier. After taking an aggressive line through the first few turns, Vonn clipped a gate just 13.4 seconds into her run, pushing her off course at the Tofane Alpine Skiing area.

The impact sent her spinning through the air before she crashed onto the icy surface, ending her race. Vonn was eventually airlifted to a hospital and diagnosed with complex fractures of the two bones in her lower left leg. Doctors put three plates and up to 20 screws in her leg, but couldn’t fix her ACL until her bones healed properly, she told Parade.

In the weeks following her initial surgery, Vonn shared that her injuries were so severe that she had to undergo an emergency fasciotomy, a procedure used to treat acute compartment syndrome. This potentially life-threatening condition occurs when pressure builds up around muscle groups, restricting blood flow or damaging muscles, tendons, and fascia. Colorado-based surgeon Dr. Tomas Hackett performed the complicated operation that saved her left leg from being amputated.

In a video shared to Instagram, the ski champion is sailing on a hydrofoil, writing that the event “was a big step in my recovery that made me incredibly happy. It may not have been snow but … it was one step closer to skiing.”

“I know I won’t be doing this, or much else, for quite some time after this next surgery, but I know I’ll get back to it,” she continued. “Eventually, I will.”

She said she expects to be on crutches for about four weeks and still has at least six months of rehabilitation in the works.

In 2010, Vonn became the first American woman to win Olympic gold in downhill skiing. Wherever she winds up, whether it’s slopeside or downhill, Vonn will always be our favorite comeback story.

Stevie Nicks Knew a Blue Tsunami Was Coming

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Like many of us, Stevie Nicks was overjoyed about the midterm election results this week, when the Democratic Party won total control of Congress.

Nicks posted a “little prayer” on Instagram, which she originally wrote on Oct. 18. “I really believed that what has happened was going to happen,” she said. “It just had to. Good almost wins…I didn’t have time to get it out before the election, so here it is. That’s the dream.”

The poem opens with Nicks describing the beauty of the early mornings, including her dog, Lily, her lamps, and her drapes. She feels anticipation for the coming day, until she remembers, “We are at war/People are starving/Right here/In the United States/We are surrounded/In violence In chaos/In hate.” But she ends on an optimistic note: “But the light creates/A circle of angels/Around this place … That’s the dream.” Trending Stories Appeals Court Says Lawsuit Over ‘The Pitt’ Can Proceed, Cites ‘Numerous’ Similarities to ‘ER’ Nicki Minaj Is Going Down With Trump’s Ship After Nearly Every Candidate She Backed Lost As Nicki Minaj Rallied for Trump's GOP, Her Lawyers Told Court She Still Owes Them $295,000 Bob Dylan Meets With Pope Leo XIV in a Private Audience

Nicks is just one of the many artists to speak out in the lead-up — and, for Dems, victorious aftermath — of the midterms. Tinashe encouraged her fans to vote (and clapped back at Republican voters), while Bruce Springsteen backed Democrat Abdul El-Sayed, who became the first Muslim senator in U.S. history.

In August, Nicks performed “Landslide” with Olivia Rodrigo at the pop star’s inaugural Daisy Chain Fields festival. She also contributed the song to Practical Magic 2 (just as she had in the original, when she re-recorded “Crystal” for the Sandra Bullock and Nicole Kidman-starring fantasy film). In January 2027, she’ll appear in Frank Marshall’s Fleetwood Mac: The Chain. It’s the first fully-authorized Fleetwood Mac documentary, coinciding with the 50th anniversary of Rumours.

The midterms proved once and for all that X is not real life

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

A week before the midterm elections, Vice President JD Vance hosted a town hall in Texas with Fox News host Jesse Watters. It was a friendly event with a friendly audience in what should have been a friendly state, and maybe that’s why Vance couldn’t help himself. When a college student earnestly asked how the right expected young people like her to settle down and have kids, given the ever-growing cost of living, Vance replied with a quip about Jon Husted’s ass.

The student, Kate White, introduced herself as the vice president of the Texas Christian University chapter of Turning Point USA, the conservative group started by Donald Trump ally Charlie Kirk. She was offering Vance an opportunity to explain how MAGA could work for young people, and he turned it into a joke. Vance did eventually answer the question, saying Republicans don’t pay enough attention to the issue of affordability and blaming rising housing costs on institutional investors and the “20 million illegal aliens” supposedly living in the United States. But the order of operations revealed the fatal flaw of Republicans’ 2026 strategy: Everything is a gag to them, and they’re way too online.

For the past decade, Republicans have adhered to Steve Bannon’s maxim that politics is downstream from culture. This fixation with liberals’ alleged capture of elite institutions has animated the right’s war against Big Tech and in particular social media platforms — which the right claims actively silenced conservative voices — and its crusade against diversity, equity, and inclusion. With his purchase of X, Elon Musk finally succeeded in building a conservative platform where right-wing voices not only had real influence but also a direct line to those in power. More recently, some people have claimed that Musk’s X is the only social media that really represents the people, unlike platforms like Bluesky, which have more left-leaning user bases.

Cocooned in their online echo chamber, MAGA figureheads convinced themselves the public was on their side, even when polling suggested otherwise. They ignored their base’s valid concerns about affordability and served them nonsensical memes instead. And they failed spectacularly in the realm where it actually mattered: politics. X, we can now declare once and for all, is not real life. Republicans not only lost historically red states like Texas, Kansas, and South Carolina, but barely managed to win Florida. The Senate race in Mississippi, of all places, is still too close to call.

Vance, especially, is a creature of X, where his following list includes such illustrious accounts as Raw Egg Nationalist, Bronze Age Pervert, Howling Mutant, and Captive Dreamer. But White House aides, department employees, and Hill staffers are stewing in the same ecosystem. Writer John Ganz has termed this phenomenon “groyperfication,” after followers of influencer Nick Fuentes, who call themselves groypers. This is a useful shorthand, though Fuentes’ fans are mostly too NEET-pilled to land jobs on the Hill. American Moment, a boot camp for young conservative up-and-comers, reportedly screens out groypers. There’s a real need for such protective measures. Kingsley Wilson, the hard-right press secretary for the Department of War (or per its legal name, Defense), has posted antisemitic conspiracy theories about Leo Frank, a Jewish man who was lynched in Georgia 1915 for allegedly raping a teenage girl. (Historians largely believe Frank was innocent.) The Department of Homeland Security regularly posts fascist fancams and wink-wink references to white supremacist memes.

Republicans saw their 2024 victory as a mandate. Having clawed the White House and Congress back from Democrats, they assumed the public was on board with MAGA’s cultural project when in fact people were largely mad about the price of eggs. And with two years of dominance under their belt, they decided to go even harder on culture, not realizing — or maybe just not caring — that people are still mad about gas prices and groceries.

Cocooned in their online echo chamber, MAGA figureheads convinced themselves the public was on their side, even when polling suggested otherwise.

In the run-up to the midterms, the Department of Education was posting about “Malfoid” — a gender-swapped version of Draco Malfoy from Harry Potter that far-right influencers claimed would save Western literature from the scourge of wokeness. The Department of Homeland Security was bragging about record lows in legal immigration, which outside white nationalist circles is typically not considered a negative thing. Pseudonymous shitposters like Howling Mutant were posting memes comparing themselves to noted child murderer Anakin Skywalker, the new avatar for the online right. Musk, a Republican megadonor, got in on the fun, as did Katie Miller, wife of ultranationalist White House adviser Stephen Miller, who dressed up as Anakin’s wife Padmé Amidala for Halloween. (Katie Miller’s last government role was advising Elon Musk’s failed Department of Government Efficiency; now she devotes her time to throwing lavish parties and hosting a podcast no one listens to.)

Most of the American public isn’t on X, but the conversations conservatives have there do bleed out into the mainstream — often with disastrous results. Bo French, the Republican candidate for Texas railroad commissioner, lost his election by a huge margin after posting anti-Indian memes on X. (Asian American voters, including Indian Americans, moved to the left in Texas elections down the ballot.) Musk did his part to drive turnout, spending nearly half a billion dollars on Republican candidates and PACs. But even Musk’s money failed to make up for voters’ dissatisfaction with Republicans’ performance, perhaps because the ads he funded focused largely on culture war issues that were out of step with voters’ concerns about the cost of living.

And Miller, it turns out, didn’t even vote, telling CBS Newsshe feared doing so would put her safety at risk, since Virginia law makes voters’ home addresses public. (The Millers, like other members of the Trump administration, live on a military installation with tight security; it’s unlikely intruders would be able to get to their home.) Republican myopia may have contributed to their poor performance across the board, including with groups that were touted as their new base. One poll found that a majority of young men of all races — including young white men — voted for Democrats in the midterms.

In a long postmortem on X — reportedly posted without input or permission from White House officials — Vance said he asked “the smartest data guy” he knows for insight on the electorate ahead of the midterms. Vance claims several pollsters told him that voters’ main concerns were the war against Iran, the cost of living, and America’s relationship to Israel. So why didn’t Republicans listen?

The answer lies in the algorithmic nature of social media, X especially. If you interact with even one post about a relatively niche topic — say, genderbent Harry Potter fanfiction — the algorithm will feed you post after post after post about the same thing. Just like Trump spent the early years of his first term obsessing over Fox News, the single mainstream network that would reflect his biases back to him, MAGA is now all in on X. And if you’re the kind of person who spends a lot of time scrolling, you may even delude yourself into thinking the issue clogging up your feed is something people just can’t stop talking about.

Ryan Giggs has urged JJ Gabriel to reconsider his decision to leave Manchester United and believes the 16-year-old should follow Max Dowman’s pathway at Arsenal.

Gabriel, who turned 16 last month, informed United of his desire to leave Old Trafford in September and has not trained with the club since then.

The young forward has attracted interest from several clubs across Europe, however, he has now reached a verbal agreement to join Real Madrid.

Gabriel’s entourage are understood to have been unhappy with his first-team integration at United and wanted him to feature in a Champions League fixture against Sabah FK, as well as in a Carabao Cup tie against Brighton in September.

The 52-year-old went on to become the most decorated player in English football history as he won 34 trophies during his 24-year career with United.

When asked about Gabriel’s situation in a conversation with Rio Ferdinand, Giggs stressed the importance of patience and believes Dowman, who was handed his Arsenal debut at 15 years and 235 days old in August 2025, is going through the correct development process under Mikel Arteta.

Saving Mass Effect 5 would cost over $200 million, BioWare co-founder says

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Join the GamesRadar community for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

Get sneak previews, exclusive competitions and details of special events each month!

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

"That is a big number to commit up front"

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

The organizers and supporters of the growing #SaveMassEffect movement have two stated goals: Get EA to commit to funding more Mass Effect games, or sell or license the IP to another developer. There are more granular achievements already happening with the accumulation of more than 300,000 signatures on Change.org, but the main ambition is right there in the hashtag circulating like a virus online: fans want to save Mass Effect, but unfortunately anyone willing and legally able will need some seriously deep pockets, according to BioWare co-founder Trent Oster.

The cancelation of Mass Effect 5 is one of the great tragedies stemming from EA's abrupt closure of beloved RPG studio BioWare, but some people, including former Dragon Age lead Mark Darrah, have suggested EA could sell franchises and license out rights to series like Mass Effect so that they can continue under new leadership. Others have cast doubt on the idea, however, and even if EA were willing to sell, it sounds like there might not be many studios with the money to buy the IP and develop a new Mass Effect.

"The scary component of Mass Effect is the audience is going to want the best of 'AAA' development," Oster says on Twitter, seemingly in response to the #SaveMassEffect movement, which he recently threw his support behind. "Rough ballpark (based on contemporaries) is $200M+. That is a big number to commit up front. There has to be a bigger plan in place for the IP to make the math work."

I love seeing all the energy around #SaveMassEffect I think there is a still a great thesis for a well-funded RPG developer who can deliver. Makes me want to get some of the original band back together.November 4, 2026

As my colleague Jasmine Gould-Wilson points out, games like Baldur's Gate 3 and Fable are proof that IP can make triumphant comebacks in the right circumstances. Wizards of the Coast handed Larian the rights to the D&D IP to make Baldur's Gate 3 and Microsoft tapped Playground Games to make Fable after shutting down original developer Lionhead in 2016, but there's a big difference with Mass Effect, and it rhymes with electronic farts. EA is famous for sitting on long-dormant IP instead of selling it, and it's given little indication that it's willing to make an exception in this case.

However, if EA were to sell the rights to Mass Effect, Oster reckons it wouldn't necessarily cost $200 million to make a new game in the series, provided the IP winds up in the right hands. "That said, I think it can be done well for less than that amount with a disciplined approach and an experienced team," he says. Let the hopium begin.

Bob Dylan Meets Pope Leo in a Black Hoodie and Polka-Dot Tie

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Yes, I was there. Not at his meeting with the pope, of course, but at the controversial Bob Dylan concert held at the Unipol Dome in Milan.

The legendary singer-songwriter has a discography that stands the test of time, but his recent decision to perform in near darkness hasn’t exactly been celebrated. With a black hood pulled up that almost completely conceals his face and only a few dim lights illuminating the stage, the impact of seeing one of the greatest artists of the 20th century was greatly diminished. From the stands, I couldn’t even zoom in on my phone because it was sealed in a bag at the entrance.

Eventually, I accepted it. Dylan intentionally created this experience, expressing his desire to distance himself from the audience. Far from a technology-forward performance, the Nobel Prize laureate, whose face has become a central figure in modern history, sang nonstop from under a hood for more than an hour and a half.

Dylan has worn the hood for at least the last two years, likely because he wants the audience to focus on his music rather than on him as a public figure. But does it have the same effect when meeting with the pope, especially when he isn’t performing?

The singer was received by Pope Leo XIV at the Apostolic Palace, dressed as if it were a stop on his tour: black sweatshirt, hood up, black gloves, black pants, and a loosely tied polka-dot tie. The ensemble was completed by an umbrella—also black—being used as a cane.

Andrea Monda, editor in chief of the Vatican newspaper L’Osservatore Romano, and Reverend Antonio Spadaro, an official from the Vatican’s Ministry of Culture, which sponsored this meeting, as it had done in July with Patti Smith, escorted Dylan. The two Americans came together in a tableau that is destined to become iconic.

The contrast bordered on the surreal: The Pope's characteristic white robes and Dylan's reclusive black hood. As always, the memes were quick to follow. Some compared Dylan to Palpatine from Star Wars, while others saw a parallel to the final encounter in the game Assassin’s Creed II.

It’s amusing to see them visually contrasted, because their personalities overlap in many ways (and not just because of their American origins). Pope Leo wrote the foreword to a book on disarmament, quoting a 1963 protest song by Dylan titled “Go Away You Bomb.” As for Dylan, he has embarked on a spiritual journey over the years that has brought him closer to the Christian faith.

Pope Leo XIV meets Bob Dylan on November 4, 2026, in Vatican City, Vatican.

“I’m a religious person,” he said in an interview with the Wall Street Journal—“I read the scriptures a lot, meditate and pray, light candles in church. I believe in damnation and salvation, as well as predestination. The Five Books of Moses, Pauline Epistles, Invocation of the Saints, all of it.”

So, is wearing a hood in the presence of the Pope a provocation? Probably not: If anything, it’s consistent with how he has made his recent public appearances.

The brief, 10-minute meeting was private, during which the two exchanged gifts. The Pope gave Dylan a rosary. The musician reciprocated with a signed copy of Chronicles–Volume One, Dylan’s autobiography. The hood stayed on throughout the meeting, turning a gesture of artistic consistency into something that, to outsiders, might look like a defensive maneuver.

Perhaps this is the intended paradox: Dylan has spent his career evading labels, yet precisely when he tries to hide, his goes viral. In many ways, it’s an act of loyalty to himself. At 85, with a career that has left an indelible mark on music and politics, he continues to defy conventions. At least he took off his gloves to shake the Pope’s hand.

(Photo by Simone Risoluti - Vatican Media via Vatican Pool/Getty Images)

Originally published by Vanity Fair Italy.

California Gov. Gavin Newsom Commutes Hundreds of Death Row Sentences

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Just days after Democrats took control of Congress, California Gov. Gavin Newsom has commuted the sentences of 548 prisoners on the state’s death row to life without parole.

“State-sanctioned killing is incompatible with California’s values, undermines the foundations of our legal system and diminishes each of us,” Newsom wrote in an open letter Thursday. “I know we cannot execute people in our imperfect legal system and remain true to our core values, first among them justice and human decency.”

Since the governor is only allowed by state law to give blanket commutations to offenders with one felony, the California Supreme Court will have to approve the commutations of those with multiple felonies — and there’s likely to be a lot of blowback.

According to the New York Times, opponents like Anne Marie Schubert, a former Sacramento district attorney, are likely to challenge the move and allege that Newsom violated the law, arguing that each case should be considered separately. Schubert, who also leads the victims rights group the Criminal Justice Legal Foundation, told the Times she plans to sue the governor, who is rumored to be mounting a 2028 presidential run.

“These are people that have suffered unimaginable losses and were given some sense of finality through the justice system,” she told the paper of victims’ families. “That is all being reopened and rehashed.”

After taking office in 2019, the governor instituted a moratorium on executions, even though the state hasn’t executed anyone in 20 years. Governor-elect Xavier Becerra, who is also a Democrat, has said he’d maintain this moratorium. Still, the state has the largest death row in the Western hemisphere, so Newsom’s decision reduces the number of people on death row in the country by about a quarter.

“This decision is among the most difficult of my career,” Newsom wrote, “not because I doubt its necessity for our state but because I know its announcement may surface excruciating memories for victims’ families.” Still, he wrote that as some cases have been “languishing” for 40 years, the finality of this move may provide comfort for some families. Editor’s picks The 250 Greatest Albums of the 21st Century So Far The 100 Best TV Episodes of All Time The 500 Greatest Albums of All Time 100 Best Movies of the 21st Century

“I won’t pretend I’ve always opposed the death penalty. I used to think the man who killed my sister deserved execution,” Jess Nichol said in a statement. Her sister, Polly Klaas, was killed in 1993 by Richard Allen Davis, who is on California’s death row. “But over time I’ve realized that whether he’s executed has absolutely nothing to do with my recovery or with real public safety. The system tells survivors that execution matters to our healing. It doesn’t. It’s a distraction from what we actually need.”

The death penalty has been in the news lately due to President Donald Trump‘s propensity for executions. Earlier this year, he announced plans to bring the firing squad back to federal prison. Defense Secretary Pete Hegseth also announced that the execution of Nidal Hasan, the Fort Hood shooter, would be livestreamed in December, before opposition caused Trump to quickly walk back that idea. Trending Stories Appeals Court Says Lawsuit Over ‘The Pitt’ Can Proceed, Cites ‘Numerous’ Similarities to ‘ER’ Nicki Minaj Is Going Down With Trump’s Ship After Nearly Every Candidate She Backed Lost As Nicki Minaj Rallied for Trump's GOP, Her Lawyers Told Court She Still Owes Them $295,000 Bob Dylan Meets With Pope Leo XIV in a Private Audience

“The silence after the very breath of a human being is violently wrestled out of them during an execution is deafening. I’ve heard it 14 times,” Reverend Jeff Hood, spiritual advisor to death row inmates, tells Rolling Stone. “Today, Governor Newsom has courageously chosen to break that silence. These commutations represent the height of moral leadership. Cruelty laid down. Now, I’m left with one prayer: God, don’t let any of the governors in the other executing states get any sleep until they find the ethical stamina to follow Newsom’s lead.”

The news comes the same day that three executions are planned in the United States, in Indiana, Florida, and Alabama. It’s the second time there have been three executions in one day in 2026. Florida has been on an execution spree since 2025, with Governor Ron DeSantis signing death warrants for 38 people to date. Critics told Rolling Stone that move is likely politically driven, with DeSantis eyeing the White House; President Donald Trump is a massive supporter of the death penalty. With the next presidential election on the horizon, it seems that the practice could be up for debate on a national level.
